		\(R=\lambda^{-1}\) and \(r_I=\alpha_I\lambda^{-1}\).  Let \(h\) be the
		signature obtained with the normalization in that lemma.  Its Walsh coefficient array
		\(\widetilde h\) has endpoints
		\((p,p)\), value \((-p,-p)\) on each complementary pair with \(B_I\ne(0,0)\),
		and zero on every pair with \(B_I=(0,0)\).
		
		Here the eight even-parity Walsh positions form the endpoint pair and
		three central pairs \(\{\one_I,\one_{I^c}\}\), where \(|I|=2\).
		In the inverse Walsh formula of \cref{eq:Walsh-coordinates}, a pair
		\(\{{\mathbf y},\bar {\mathbf y}\}\) with common coefficient \(b\) contributes
		\[
		b\bigl((-1)^{{\mathbf x}\cdot {\mathbf y}}+(-1)^{{\mathbf x}\cdot\bar {\mathbf y}}\bigr).
		\]
		This vanishes when \(\wt({\mathbf x})\) is odd.  At either endpoint all signs are
		positive.  At \({\mathbf x}=\one_J\), \(|J|=2\), the endpoint pair and the
		pair \(\{\one_J,\one_{J^c}\}\) have positive signs, and the other two
		central pairs have negative signs.  Thus, if exactly \(k\) of the three pairs \(B_I\) are
		nonzero,
		\[
		\begin{aligned}
			h(\zero^4)=h(\one^4)&=2p(1-k),\\
			h(\one_J)&=
			\begin{cases}
				2p(k-1),&\text{if \(B_J\ne(0,0)\)},\\
				2p(k+1),&\text{if \(B_J=(0,0)\)}.
			\end{cases}
		\end{aligned}
		\]
		Walsh inversion gives the K-coordinate entries of the normalized gadget
		output. Thus \(k=1\) gives a
		nonzero multiple of \(X\otimes X\), up to a variable permutation, whose
		factorization pulls back through the invertible binary attachments just
		constructed.  For
		\(k=0\), the original Walsh tensor is already a higher-arity CP signature, so
		\cref{lem:walsh-ge-terminal} gives outcome~1.  For \(k=2,3\), the output in
		K-coordinates has even-parity support and two nonzero endpoints.  Add this quaternary and apply \cref{cor:eight-vertex-complete-closure}.  Its
		conclusion transfers to \(\cF\) by the gadget
		reduction and preservation under taking subsets, again giving outcome~1.
		
		It remains that \(p=t=0\).  If just one complementary pair has nonzero coefficients,
		\cref{lem:walsh-ge-terminal} gives outcome~1 directly.  Otherwise label the three complementary pairs
		\(\{\one_{I_\ell},\one_{I_\ell^c}\}\), \(\ell=1,2,3\).
		Write each nonzero pair \(B_I\) as
		\(\rho_I(1,\alpha_I)\), choose the unique \(\beta_I\in\mu_N\) with
		\(\beta_I^2=\alpha_I\). For a zero pair choose \(\beta_I\in\mu_N\)
		arbitrarily.  Apply \cref{lem:odd-cyc-four-port-dressing} with
		\(R=1,r_I=\beta_I\).  Write \(h\) for the resulting signature, rescaled according to
		\cref{lem:odd-cyc-four-port-dressing}.  Its nonzero Walsh coefficient pairs
		become \((\sigma_I,\sigma_I)\), where
		\(\sigma_I=\rho_I\beta_I\). Set \(\sigma_I=0\) when \(B_I=(0,0)\), and write
		\(\sigma_\ell=\sigma_{I_\ell}\).  These attachments again give an invertible operation on each variable.  Put \(c=\sigma_1+\sigma_2+\sigma_3\).  The same sign
		calculation, now with zero Walsh endpoints, gives
		\[
		\begin{aligned}
			h(\zero^4)=h(\one^4)&=2c,\\
			h(\one_{I_\ell})=h(\one_{I_\ell^c})
			&=2(2\sigma_\ell-c)\qquad(\ell=1,2,3),
		\end{aligned}
		\]
		and all odd-weight entries vanish.  If \(c\ne0\), this is a
		complement-invariant mountain. After division by \(2c\), its endpoints
		are one and its three central coefficient-pair values are
		\[
		r_\ell=2\sigma_\ell/c-1,
		\qquad 1+r_1+r_2+r_3=1+2c/c-3=0.
		\]
		Up to the nonzero scalar, this mountain is the output of invertible binary
		attachments to \(q\), so it may be
		added by \cref{eq:finite-equivalent-enlargement}.
		\Cref{cor:eight-vertex-complete-closure} makes the enlarged problem either
		tractable or \(\SharpP\)-hard.  Preservation under taking subsets in the first case and
		the gadget reduction in the second transfer that conclusion to
		\(\cF\), giving outcome~1.
		
		Assume \(c=0\).  Exactly one nonzero pair is impossible.  Two nonzero Walsh pairs have
		values \(\sigma,-\sigma\). After permuting variables, \(\widetilde h\) factors as
		\[
		\sigma
		\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\-1&0\end{pmatrix}_{14}\otimes
		\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\-1&0\end{pmatrix}_{23},
		\]
		so the factor-reduction equivalence applies.  Finally suppose that all three \(\sigma_i\)
		are nonzero.  Choose distinct \(i,j,k\) with
		\(\{i,j,k\}=\{1,2,3\}\) and \(\sigma_j^2+\sigma_k^2\ne0\), which exists since otherwise subtraction
		would give \(2\sigma_i^2=0\).  Put pair \(i\) at \(12\mid34\), and join
		variables \(3,4\) of one copy to variables \(1,2\) of another by edges.  The
		output is
		\begin{equation*}
			\frac14\widetilde G_i=
			\begin{pmatrix}
				\sigma_i^2&0&0&0\\
				0&-(\sigma_j^2+\sigma_k^2)&-2\sigma_j\sigma_k&0\\
				0&-2\sigma_j\sigma_k&-(\sigma_j^2+\sigma_k^2)&0\\
				0&0&0&\sigma_i^2
			\end{pmatrix}.
		\end{equation*}
		If every phase contraction of \(G_i\) were zero or had transfer class in
		\(C_N\) in Walsh coordinates, its equal nonzero endpoints and
		\cref{lem:odd-cyc-q4-family} would give \(\lambda=1\). Complement symmetry
		and odd \(N\) then force every nonzero central coefficient to be
		\(-\sigma_i^2\).  In particular,
		\[
		\sigma_j^2+\sigma_k^2=\sigma_i^2=(\sigma_j+\sigma_k)^2,
		\]
		because \(\sigma_i=-(\sigma_j+\sigma_k)\), contradicting
		\(\sigma_j\sigma_k\ne0\).  Hence a phase contraction of \(G_i\) is a
		nonzero binary which is singular or has Walsh transfer class outside
		\(C_N\).  A singular binary gives unary factors and the odd-arity theorem.
		A nonsingular binary of infinite projective transfer order is handled by
		\cref{lem:binary-interpolation}.  Otherwise its transfer has finite
		projective order, contradicting the definition of \(G(\cF)\).
		Every hardness or tractability conclusion above gives outcome~1 through the
		cited theorem and the equivalent-enlargement reduction.  In a
		factor case, the attached binaries act invertibly, so the displayed
		factorization pulls back to \(q\). The transformed mountain excludes the
		common-one-sided exception, and the factor-reduction equivalence gives the
		reduction in outcome~2.
	\end{proof}
	
	\begin{proof}[Completion of \cref{thm:odd-cyclic}]
		Apply \cref{lem:height-one-card-saturation}.  Its first two outcomes are the
		first two conclusions of the theorem.  In outcome~3, the listed two-variable
		contractions and all their irreducible factors are retained
		in the equivalent set supplied by outcome~3.
		Other fixed gadget outputs and any required factors are added separately
		by \cref{eq:finite-equivalent-enlargement,thm:external-factor}, as in the
		quaternary closure proof.  If a nonbinary irreducible signature remains, choose one of minimum arity.  The argument preceding
		\cref{lem:odd-cyc-one-matching} proves
		condition~\ref{cond:height-one-contractions} for this signature.
		\Cref{thm:odd-cyc-higher-collapse} bounds its arity by four, and
		\cref{thm:odd-cyc-quaternary-closure} then gives hardness, tractability, or
		a tensor factorization.  The last possibility contradicts the choice of an
		irreducible signature, so outcome~1 holds.
		
		In the remaining case every irreducible signature is binary.
		By \cref{lem:height-one-card-saturation}\textnormal{(3)}, each has transfer
		class in \(G(\cF)\) and Walsh tensor proportional to \(D(r)Z\).
		Hence \cref{eq:common-Walsh-product-presentation} holds,
		which proves the third conclusion of \cref{thm:odd-cyclic}.
	\end{proof}
	

\subsection{Completion of the height-one case}

	We now combine the binary reduction and the two group cases into one
	step of the iteration. Its alternatives distinguish a completed
	classification from a strict update to which the same argument applies.

	\begin{theorem}[Hardness, tractability, or a strict update]
		\label{thm:height-one-transfer-interface}
		Let \(\cF_0\) be the initial signature set, and let
		\((A,\cF)\) be a stage of the sequence in
		\cref{lem:normalization-ledger}. In particular,
		\[
		A\cF_0\subseteq\cF,
		\qquad A^{\mathsf T}XA=\mu_AX\quad(\mu_A\ne0),
		\qquad \KHolant(\cF)\equivT\KHolant(\cF_0).
		\]
		Suppose \(\cF\) is finite, preprocessed, algebraic, and factor-closed,
		and contains the height-one mountain
		\[
		B=\begin{pmatrix}c_0&u\\v&c_1\end{pmatrix},
		\qquad c_0c_1\ne0.
		\]
		Then one of the following occurs:
		\begin{enumerate}
			\item \(\KHolant(\cF)\) is \(\SharpP\)-hard, or
			\(\Tract(K\cF)\) holds.
			\item there are \(A^+\in GO(X)\) and a finite preprocessed algebraic
			factor-closed set \(\cF^+\), continuing the same
			sequence of additions and changes of basis, such that
			\[
			A^+\cF_0\subseteq\cF^+,
			\qquad
			(A^+)^{\mathsf T}XA^+=\mu_{A^+}X\quad(\mu_{A^+}\ne0),
			\qquad
			\KHolant(\cF^+)\equivT\KHolant(\cF),
			\]
			and, for \(D=A^+A^{-1}\), the transformed binary
			\(DBD^{\mathsf T}\) remains in \(\cF^+\) up to its known nonzero
			scalar.  In this alternative every nonzero directly realizable ordered
			binary over both \(\cF\) and \(\cF^+\) is nonsingular with
			finite projective transfer order, so
			\(G(\cF)\) and \(G(\cF^+)\) are defined.  Moreover, either
			\[
			[A]^{-1}G(\cF)[A]
			\subsetneq
			[A^+]^{-1}G(\cF^+)[A^+],
			\]
			or both values of \(\nu\) are defined and
			\[
			\nu(\cF^+)<\nu(\cF).
			\]
			Both quantities satisfy the non-strict comparisons and fixed-field
			conditions of \cref{def:height-one-outer-successor}.
		\end{enumerate}
	\end{theorem}
	
	\begin{proof}
		Maintain factor closure using the factor-reduction equivalence. The
		retained mountain excludes the common-one-sided exception. Each
		nontrivial factorization produces positive-arity factors of strictly smaller
		arity, so this process terminates.  An odd-arity member is handled by
        \cref{thm:external-odd,eq:K-equivalence}. A rank-one binary supplies unaries and hence the same
		conclusion.  If no nonbinary irreducible signature remains, the
		arity-at-most-two alternative gives tractability.
		
		All additions of signatures and global changes of basis satisfy
		\cref{lem:normalization-ledger}.  Whenever the groups are defined, conjugating them
		back to the original basis gives nested groups.  The old irreducible signatures, up to one common
		invertible transformation, remain in the set, so \(\nu\) cannot increase.  A strict
		group inclusion or decrease of \(\nu\) gives outcome~2.  If adding signatures preserves both quantities, the matrix and group
		remain unchanged.
		\Cref{lem:height-one-card-saturation} proves separately that the required
		recursive contraction and factor extraction is finite.
		
		If \(B\) is singular, \cref{lem:binary-interpolation} supplies its unary
		factors. If \(BX\) is nonsingular of infinite projective
		order, it first supplies a rank-one binary and then unaries.  The same argument applies to every other directly realizable binary that
		is singular or whose nonsingular transfer has infinite projective order.  We may therefore assume
		that every
		nonzero directly realizable binary is nonsingular and of finite projective transfer order.
		Then \cref{lem:bounded-torsion,thm:finite-group-split} give either
		a directly realizable nonsingular weighted equality or, after one
		\(GO(X)\)-normalization, an odd cyclic or odd dihedral residual form.
		In the equality case, write \(E=\diag(p,q)\), where \(pq\ne0\).
		The gadget gives \(\KHolant(\cF,E)\leT\KHolant(\cF)\).
		Choose a diagonal \(D\in GO(X)\) with \(DED^{\mathsf T}\doteq I\).
		The set \(D(\cF\cup\{E\})\) satisfies the hypotheses of
		\cref{thm:eq2-kholant-dichotomy}.  Its hard conclusion composes back to
		\(\cF\), while its tractable conclusion pulls back through the problem
		equivalence and \cref{lem:normalization-ledger}. Hence this is outcome~1.
		
		Apply \cref{thm:odd-cyclic,thm:odd-dihedral} in the two residual forms.
		Their first conclusions give outcome~1, and their updates satisfy
		outcome~2 by \cref{def:height-one-outer-successor}.  Their remaining
		conclusions give \cref{eq:common-Walsh-product-presentation}, which
		\cref{lem:normalization-ledger} implies \(\Tract(K\cF)\).
		This again gives outcome~1 and completes the case analysis.
	\end{proof}
	
	\begin{proof}[Proof of \cref{cor:height-one-complete}]
		Maintain \((A,\cF)\) and the invariant of
		\cref{lem:normalization-ledger} over the fixed initial number field, and
		apply \cref{thm:height-one-transfer-interface}.  Its first outcome is the
		required hardness or tractability conclusion.  The second outcome gives
		an update satisfying \cref{def:height-one-outer-successor}. By
		\cref{lem:height-one-progress-well-founded}, only finitely many such
		updates are possible.  Between these updates, factor closure terminates by
		strict arity descent, and the recursive contractions and factor extraction
		terminate by \cref{lem:height-one-card-saturation}.  Repeated application
		therefore reaches the first outcome.  Finally,
		\cref{lem:normalization-ledger} gives the conclusion for the
		initial signature set.
	\end{proof}
	
	\section{Mountain Height Two}
	\label{sec:height-two}
	
	
	\begin{theorem}[Dichotomy with a height-two mountain]
		\label{cor:height-two-complete}
		Let \(\cF\) be a finite algebraic signature set containing a height-two
		mountain.  Then
		\[
		\Tract(K\cF)
		\qquad\text{or}\qquad
		\KHolant(\cF)\text{ is }\SharpP\text{-hard}.
		\]
	\end{theorem}
	
	\begin{proof}
		A height-two mountain has arity four, is supported on Hamming weights
		\(0,2,4\), and has two nonzero endpoint values. It is therefore an
		eight-vertex signature satisfying the hypotheses of
		\cref{cor:eight-vertex-complete-closure}, which gives the conclusion.
	\end{proof}
	
	\Needspace{10\baselineskip}
	\section{Mountain Height Three}
	\label{sec:height-at-least-three}
	
	\begin{theorem}[Height-three mountain dichotomy]
		\label{thm:height-three-complete}
		Let \(\cF\) be a finite algebraic set of even-arity signatures such that
		\(\chi_r({\mathbf x})\in3\mathbb Z\) for every arity-\(r\) member \(f\in\cF\)
		and every \({\mathbf x}\in\supp(f)\).  Suppose that \(\cF\) contains a mountain
		\[
		M=a\delta_{\zero^6}+b\delta_{\one^6}+Q,
		\qquad ab\ne0,\qquad Q\text{ EO}.
		\]
		Then \(\Tract(K\cF)\) holds or \(\KHolant(\cF)\) is
		\(\SharpP\)-hard.
	\end{theorem}
	
	Write \(Q=M|_{\EO}\) for the central restriction. We construct a nonzero
	quaternary EO signature \(h\notin\mathcal B_\times\), where
	\(\mathcal B_\times\) is the class of products of nonsingular
	anti-diagonal binary signatures defined below, and obtain
	\(\KHolant(\cF,h)\leT\KHolant(\cF)\).
	Since \(\mathcal B_{\rm ARS}\subseteq\mathcal B_\times\),
	\cref{thm:hc-relative-eo-terminal} then gives hardness in the remaining
	case \(\neg\Tract(K\cF)\).
	If \(Q\in\mathcal B_\times\), a two-copy construction supplies
	\(h\). Otherwise, we use self-loops and two-copy contractions, together
	with the computer-assisted exhaustive classification in Appendix~\ref{proofsubsec:cert-high-charge-q6}.
	
	\subsection{Matching Products and Two-Copy Constructions}
	
	Let \(V\) be an ordered set of even cardinality.  For distinct \(i,j\in V\),
	write \(\partial_{ij}f\) for the contraction with an \(X\)-edge.
	In the following formula, the first two displayed arguments assign values
	to variables \(i,j\), respectively, and all other variables retain their order:
	\[
	(\partial_{ij}f)({\mathbf x}_{V\setminus\{i,j\}})
	=f(0,1,{\mathbf x}_{V\setminus\{i,j\}})
	+f(1,0,{\mathbf x}_{V\setminus\{i,j\}}).
	\]
	Define the matching-product class
	\[
	\mathcal B_\times(V)=
	\left\{\lambda\bigotimes_{\{u,v\}\in P}
	\begin{pmatrix}0&p_{uv}\\q_{uv}&0\end{pmatrix}_{u,v}:
	\begin{array}{l}
		P\text{ is a perfect matching of }V,\\
		\lambda\prod_{\{u,v\}\in P}p_{uv}q_{uv}\ne0
	\end{array}\right\},
	\]
	and put \(\mathcal B_\times^0=\mathcal B_\times\cup\{0\}\).
	Each factor has a specified orientation. Swapping its two variables exchanges its
	two nonzero entries.  In particular,
	\(\mathcal B_{\rm ARS}\subseteq\mathcal B_\times\).

	
	
	\begin{lemma}[A matching-product central restriction yields a quaternary signature]
		\label{lem:hc-transversal-cube-terminal}
		Let \(d\ge3\), and let \(\cF\) be a finite algebraic signature set
		containing
		\[
		M=a\delta_{\zero^{2d}}+b\delta_{\one^{2d}}+Q,
		\qquad ab\ne0,
		\]
		where, for a perfect matching
		\(P=\{\{u_1,v_1\},\ldots,\{u_d,v_d\}\}\) of the ordered variables,
		\[
		Q=\lambda\bigotimes_{k=1}^{d}
		\begin{pmatrix}0&p_k\\q_k&0\end{pmatrix}_{u_k,v_k},
		\qquad \lambda\prod_{k=1}^{d}p_kq_k\ne0.
		\]
		There is a fixed two-copy gadget over \(\{M\}\), using only \(X\)-edges, whose ordered boundary signature \(h\) is EO and has the six
		weight-two entries
		\begin{equation}
		\bigl(h_{0011},h_{0101},h_{0110},h_{1001},h_{1010},h_{1100}\bigr)
		=(ab,c_1,c_2,c_3,c_4,ab),
		\qquad c_1c_2c_3c_4\ne0.
		\label{eq:auto-wt-18a}
		\end{equation}
		In particular,
		\[
		\KHolant(\cF,h)\leT\KHolant(\cF).
		\]
		The signature \(h\) is not a tensor product of two nonsingular
		anti-diagonal binaries.  Moreover, its supported triples include
		\[
		1100\oplus1010\oplus0110=0000,
		\qquad
		0011\oplus0101\oplus1001=1111.
		\]
	\end{lemma}
	
	\begin{proof}
		We connect two copies so that their central matchings and the connecting
		edges form two odd-length alternating components. These supply four
		boundary patterns with one bit equal to one on each external pair,
		while the two endpoint choices supply the other two patterns.
		Write the matching of \(Q\) as
		\(P=\{\{w_0,w_1\},\ldots,\{w_{2d-2},w_{2d-1}\}\}\).  On two copies of
		the mountain, define a variable bijection \(p\) by fixing
		\(w_0,w_{2d-1}\) and transposing
		\[
		(w_1,w_2),(w_3,w_4),\ldots,(w_{2d-3},w_{2d-2}).
		\]
		Leave \((w_0,w_1)\) external on the first copy and \((w_0,w_2)\)
		external on the second, in the external order
		\((w_0^{(1)},w_1^{(1)},w_0^{(2)},w_2^{(2)})\).  For every
		\(r\notin\{0,1\}\), join the
		first-copy variable \(w_r\) to the second-copy variable \(p(w_r)\) by one \(X\)-edge.  With both copies central, the matching edges and the connecting X-edges form
		two alternating components: the edge between the first external pair and
		a path of length $4d-3$ between the second.  Both lengths are odd, so each
		weight-one/weight-one boundary pattern has a unique extension and a
		nonzero coefficient $c_i$.
		
		If both copies use endpoints, the edges force the two constant strings
		to be complementary.  This gives exactly the other two EO patterns, with
		unique extensions of value \(ab\).  A mixed endpoint--central extension is
		impossible: for $d\ge3$, some central matching edge has both variables connected to
		the constant copy and is forced equal.  Thus each nonzero coefficient in \eqref{eq:auto-wt-18a} has exactly one nonzero extension.
		
		A quaternary member of \(\mathcal B_\times\) has exactly four supported
		strings, so the six-string support excludes membership in this class.
		It also contains the two triples displayed in the statement, whose xor
		identities follow directly.
	\end{proof}

The first identity rules out the positive sign and the second rules out
		the negative sign in the support clause of \(\EOTract\).  Hence
		\(\neg\EOTract(\{h\})\), and \cref{thm:external-eo} makes
		\(\KHolant(\{h\})\) \(\SharpP\)-hard.  Since
		\[
		\KHolant(\{h\})
		\leT\KHolant(\cF,h)
		\leT\KHolant(\cF),
		\]
		the problem \(\KHolant(\cF)\) is \(\SharpP\)-hard.
	
	\subsection{Arity-Six Loop and Two-Copy Contractions}
	\label{subsec:hc-q6-base}
	
	The fifteen self-loop contractions alone do not force the required
	restriction on \(Q\), as the counterexample in
	\cref{rem:cert-high-charge-q6-one-loop-insufficient} shows. We therefore
also use two-copy contractions. If every output belongs to
\(\mathcal B_\times^0\), the finite classification in
\cref{thm:cert-high-charge-q6-interface} forces the support of \(Q\) into
one complementary pair. The calculation and its solver dependence are
given in \cref{prop:cert-high-charge-q6-joint-deck}.
	
	\begin{definition}[Loop and two-copy contractions of an arity-six mountain]
		\label{def:hc-q6-joint-deck}
		Let \(U=[6]\), and let
		\[
		M=a\delta_{\zero^6}+b\delta_{\one^6}+Q,
		\qquad
		Q=\sum_{S\in\binom U3}q_S\delta_{\one_S},
		\qquad ab\ne0.
		\]
		For each unordered pair \(\{i,j\}\in\binom U2\), let
		\(\partial_{ij}M\) be the quaternary EO signature obtained by one
		\(X\)-self-loop, with the residual variables in increasing order.  For
		\(R\in\binom{U\setminus\{i,j\}}2\),
		\begin{equation}
			(\partial_{ij}M)(\one_R)=q_{R\cup\{i\}}+q_{R\cup\{j\}}.
			\label{eq:hc-q6-loop-deck}
		\end{equation}
		These are the fifteen one-copy contractions.
		
		For a two-copy contraction, choose unordered two-element external sets
		\(A,B\in\binom U2\), list them in increasing order
		\[
		A=(a_0,a_1),
		\qquad
		B=(b_0,b_1),
		\]
		and retain the first/second labels of the two copies of \(M\).
		Put \(I_A=U\setminus A\) and \(I_B=U\setminus B\), and choose a
		bijection \(\pi:I_A\to I_B\).  Leave \(A\) external on the first copy
		and \(B\) external on the second copy, in the order
		\((a_0,a_1,b_0,b_1)\), and join every \(r\in I_A\) to
		\(\pi(r)\in I_B\) by an \(X\)-edge.  For
		\({\mathbf x},{\mathbf y}\in\{0,1\}^2\), put
		\[
		A_{\mathbf x}=\{a_r:x_r=1\},
		\qquad
		B_{\mathbf y}=\{b_r:y_r=1\}.
		\]
		The resulting quaternary EO signature \(C_{A,B,\pi}\) has, when
		\(\wt({\mathbf x})+\wt({\mathbf y})=2\),
		\begin{equation}
			C_{A,B,\pi}({\mathbf x},{\mathbf y})
			=ab\,\one[({\mathbf x},{\mathbf y})\in\{(00,11),(11,00)\}]
			+\sum_{\substack{T\subseteq I_A\\|T|=3-\wt({\mathbf x})}}
			q_{A_{\mathbf x}\cup T}\,
			q_{B_{\mathbf y}\cup(I_B\setminus\pi(T))}.
			\label{eq:hc-q6-two-copy-deck}
		\end{equation}
		The first term comes from complementary endpoint choices on the two
		copies, and the sum comes from two central assignments. A mixed choice
		would force four variables of the central copy to be equal, which is
		incompatible with its weight-three support. The endpoint choices leave
		two external ones, as do two central assignments after the four internal
		\(X\)-edges each account for one bit equal to one. Thus the output is EO.
		There are \(15^2\cdot4!=5400\) labelled two-copy contractions.
	\end{definition}
	
	\begin{lemma}[Support forced by arity-six contractions]
		\label{lem:hc-q6-joint-rigidity}
		Let \(M\) and its contractions \(\partial_{ij}M\), \(C_{A,B,\pi}\) be as in
		\cref{def:hc-q6-joint-deck}.  Suppose that every one of the fifteen
		signatures \(\partial_{ij}M\) and every one of the \(5400\) labelled
		signatures \(C_{A,B,\pi}\) belongs to
		\(\mathcal B_\times^0([4])\) on its four ordered dangling edges.  Then
		\begin{equation}
		\operatorname{supp}(Q)\subseteq\{\one_S,\one_{U\setminus S}\}
		\qquad\text{for some }S\in\binom U3.
		\label{eq:auto-q6-5}
		\end{equation}
	\end{lemma}
	
	\begin{proof}
		With \(t=ab\), the signatures \(\partial_{ij}M\) and \(C_{A,B,\pi}\) in
		\cref{eq:hc-q6-loop-deck,eq:hc-q6-two-copy-deck} are precisely the loop
		and labelled two-copy arrays covered by
		\cref{thm:cert-high-charge-q6-interface}.  It applies to arbitrary complex \(q_S\)
        and every \(ab\ne0\), and gives \eqref{eq:auto-q6-5}.
	\end{proof}
	
	\begin{theorem}[A quaternary EO output outside \(\mathcal B_\times\) from an arity-six mountain]
		\label{thm:hc-q6-complete}
		Let \(\cF\) be a finite algebraic signature set containing
		\[
		M=a\delta_{\zero^6}+b\delta_{\one^6}+Q,
		\qquad ab\ne0,
		\qquad Q\text{ EO}.
		\]
		There is a nonzero quaternary EO signature
		\(h\notin\mathcal B_\times\) such that
		\[
		\KHolant(\cF,h)\leT\KHolant(\cF).
		\]
		The signature \(h\) can be chosen as one of the fifteen one-copy contractions
		\(\partial_{ij}M\), one of the \(5400\) labelled two-copy contractions
		\(C_{A,B,\pi}\) from \cref{def:hc-q6-joint-deck}, or the explicit
		two-copy output of \cref{lem:hc-transversal-cube-terminal}.
	\end{theorem}
	
	\begin{proof}
		Suppose first that \(Q\in\mathcal B_\times\). Write
		\[
		Q=\lambda\bigotimes_{r=0}^2
		\begin{pmatrix}0&p_r\\q_r&0\end{pmatrix}_{w_{2r},w_{2r+1}},
		\qquad \lambda p_0q_0p_1q_1p_2q_2\ne0.
		\]
		The case \(d=3\) of \cref{lem:hc-transversal-cube-terminal} gives the
		six-string output \eqref{eq:auto-wt-18a}, with one nonzero extension per
		supported string. Choose this output as \(h\).
		
		Now suppose \(Q\notin\mathcal B_\times\). If \(Q=0\), a contraction
		\(C_{A,B,\pi}\) has exactly the two endpoint--endpoint coefficients
		\(ab\). Its support has size two, whereas a quaternary member of
		\(\mathcal B_\times\) has support size four. Choose this contraction as \(h\).
		It remains to consider \(Q\ne0\). If a loop or two-copy contraction
		lies outside \(\mathcal B_\times^0\), choose it as \(h\).
		Otherwise \cref{lem:hc-q6-joint-rigidity} gives
		\(\operatorname{supp}(Q)\subseteq\{\one_S,\one_{U\setminus S}\}\).
		Choose \(i\in S\) and \(j\notin S\). The two possible nonzero
        coefficients of \(\partial_{ij}M\) have the unique extensions
        \(\one_S,\one_{U\setminus S}\). Hence this contraction is nonzero with support at
		most two, contradicting its membership in \(\mathcal B_\times^0\).
		
		In each case, the selected \(h\) is the output of a direct gadget over
		\(\{M\}\), so \cref{eq:direct-gadget-reduction} gives the required reduction.
	\end{proof}
	
	
	\begin{proof}[Proof of \cref{thm:height-three-complete}]
		Apply zero/nullary preprocessing and assume \(\neg\Tract(K\cF)\).
		By \cref{thm:hc-q6-complete}, there is a nonzero quaternary EO signature
		\(h\notin\mathcal B_\times\) with
		\(\KHolant(\cF\cup\{h\})\leT\KHolant(\cF)\).
		The finite algebraic set \(\cF\cup\{h\}\) retains the mountain, whose
		endpoints witness both charge signs. The added signature has charge zero,
		so the charge divisibility condition is also preserved.
		By \cref{lem:downward-inheritance}, \(\Tract(K(\cF\cup\{h\}))\) fails.
		Since \(\mathcal B_{\rm ARS}\subseteq\mathcal B_\times\), we have
		\(h\notin\mathcal B_{\rm ARS}\). Thus
		\cref{thm:hc-relative-eo-terminal} applies to \(\cF\cup\{h\}\), and the
		preceding reduction transfers hardness to the original \(\cF\).
	\end{proof}
	
	\section{Mountain Height at Least Four}
	\label{sec:height-at-least-four}
	
	\begin{theorem}[Mountain dichotomy at every height at least four]
		\label{thm:height-at-least-four}
		Let \(d\ge4\), and let \(\cF\) be a finite algebraic set of even-arity
		signatures such that
		\[
		\chi_r({\mathbf x})\in d\mathbb Z
		\qquad(f\in\cF,\ \arity(f)=r,\ {\mathbf x}\in\supp(f)).
		\]
		Suppose that \(\cF\) contains a mountain
		\[
		M=a\delta_{\zero^{2d}}+b\delta_{\one^{2d}}+Q,
		\qquad ab\ne0,\qquad Q\text{ EO}.
		\]
		Then \(\Tract(K\cF)\) holds or \(\KHolant(\cF)\) is
		\(\SharpP\)-hard.
	\end{theorem}
	
	An \emph{\(X\)-matching product} is a nonzero scalar multiple of
	\(\bigotimes_{e\in P}X_e\), where \(P\) is a perfect matching of its
	variables. Such binary factors are irreducible, so
	uniqueness of irreducible factorization identifies both their variable pairs
	and their projective values.
	
	We first normalize the complete mountain so that all pair contractions
	of its central restriction are \(X\)-matching products. A common scalar
	then makes that restriction real-valued and complement-invariant, allowing
	the reconstruction theorem of Shao and Cai to apply. For \(d\ge5\), it
	gives a matching-product central restriction, and the two-copy construction
	of \cref{lem:hc-transversal-cube-terminal} finishes the case.
	At height four, the same conclusion holds when the contraction matchings
	satisfy the overlap condition below. Otherwise we recover the Reed--Muller
	central restriction, normalize its endpoint coefficients, and obtain
	equality by a Turing reduction.
	
	\begin{remark}[Relation to the real-valued Holant argument]
		\label{rem:sc20-high-height}
		The normalization by attaching binary signatures below follows the contraction and binary-attachment
		strategy of \cite[full version, Lemma~8.1]{ShaoCai2020}.  Their transformed
		signature set satisfies ARS globally.  Here the signature set is
		complex-valued: charge conservation makes the self-loop outputs EO, and
		\cref{thm:hc-relative-eo-terminal} forces their binary ARS factorization
		unless hardness has already been proved.  This supplies the factorization needed for the binary attachments.
		
        The constructions in \cref{lem:hc-port-normalization} use the complete
        mountain and its contractions. Together with the phase recovery in
        \cref{lem:hc-global-phase}, they establish the hypotheses of
        \cref{lem:sc-x-product-reconstruction} for the central restriction.
	\end{remark}
	
	\subsection{Common Normalization and Phase Recovery}
	\label{subsec:hc-normalization}
	
	For the proof of \cref{thm:height-at-least-four}, apply zero/nullary
	preprocessing and assume \(\neg\Tract(K\cF)\).
	If \(Q=0\), \cref{lem:pure-ge} already gives hardness.  Hence it suffices to
	consider \(Q\ne0\).
	Every self-loop on \(M\) kills its endpoints:
	\begin{equation}
	\partial_{ij}M=\partial_{ij}Q.
	\label{eq:hc-central-card}
	\end{equation}
	More generally, charge conservation keeps every supported half-charge
	in \(d\mathbb Z\). For an output of positive arity less than \(2d\), its
	absolute value is less than \(d\), so it must be zero. Such outputs are EO.  By
	\cref{thm:hc-relative-eo-terminal}, any such nonzero output outside
	\(\mathcal B_{\rm ARS}\) gives hardness.  Otherwise its factors are
	nonsingular binaries
	\[
	B(z)=\begin{pmatrix}0&z\\\bar z&0\end{pmatrix},
	\qquad z\in\AlgNums^\times,
	\]
	up to nonzero scalars.
	These factors are available by \cref{thm:external-factor}: the mountain
	is two-sided, excluding the common-one-sided exception.  The constructions
	below use finitely many such factors and compose their reductions.
	The extracted binaries have charge zero, and
	\cref{lem:downward-inheritance} excludes tractability for the corresponding
	oracle problems.
	
	To normalize a selected binary factor to \(X\), we attach a copy of that
	factor to one of its variables. Attaching the first variable of an ordered \(B(z)\) to a mountain variable through
	\(X\) acts on that variable by
	\begin{equation}
	\begin{aligned}
T_{B(z)^{\mathsf T}}&=B(z)^{\mathsf T}X=\diag(\bar z,z)=\bar zD(z/\bar z),\\
T_{B(z)^{\mathsf T}}B(z)&=|z|^2X.
\end{aligned}
	\label{eq:hc-port-mating}
	\end{equation}
	This invertible diagonal map preserves support and both nonzero endpoints.
	To preserve a previously normalized contraction, we use the following
	criterion for consistently oriented factors:
	\begin{equation}
	B(z)^{\mathsf T}XB(w)\doteq X
	\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
	w/z\in\mathbb R^\times
	\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad B(w)\doteq B(z).
	\label{eq:hc-binary-splice}
	\end{equation}
	Indeed the two off-diagonal entries are \(\bar z w\) and \(z\bar w\).
	In this case \(T_{B(z)^{\mathsf T}}^{\mathsf T}XT_{B(w)^{\mathsf T}}\) is also a nonzero scalar multiple of
	\(X\).  All these operations use the extracted ordered binaries on the
	complete mountain.  
	
	\begin{lemma}[Vanishing pair contractions]
		\label{lem:wt-zero-loop}
		Let \(h\) have arity at least three.  If \(\partial_{ij}h=0\) for every
		pair \(i\ne j\), then \(h({\mathbf x})=0\) whenever
		\(0<\wt({\mathbf x})<\arity(h)\).
		In particular, an EO signature with all pair contractions zero is zero.
	\end{lemma}
	
	\begin{proof}
		This is \cite[Lemma~2.7]{CaiFuShao2020ARS}. Its three-equation
		argument is purely linear and uses neither ARS nor nonvanishing contractions.
	\end{proof}
	
	We also write \(\partial_e\) when \(e=\{i,j\}\).
	
	The next lemma propagates the normalized form from selected contractions
	to all pair contractions of the same EO tensor.
	\begin{lemma}[Propagation of \(X\)-matching products]
		\label{lem:hc-x-line-propagation}
		Let \(F\) be EO on an even variable set \(V\), with \(|V|\ge8\), and suppose
		every nonzero \(\partial_eF\) lies in \(\mathcal B_{\rm ARS}\).
		Either of the following conditions implies that every \(\partial_eF\)
		is an \(X\)-matching product.
		\begin{enumerate}
			\item Every \(\partial_eF\) with \(e\cap\{1,2\}=\varnothing\) is an
			\(X\)-matching product.
			\item Both \(\partial_{12}F\) and \(\partial_{ij}F\) are
			\(X\)-matching products,
			\(\{1,2\}\cap\{i,j\}=\varnothing\), and the matching of
			\(\partial_{ij}F\) pairs \(1,2\) with distinct mates \(u,v\).
		\end{enumerate}
		The remaining pair contractions are allowed to be zero in the hypotheses.
	\end{lemma}
	
	\begin{proof}
		Contracting any pair in an \(X\)-matching product gives another nonzero
		\(X\)-matching product: it either removes a matching edge with scalar
		\(2\), or splices two edges with scalar \(1\).  Thus, whenever a deleted
		pair \(e\) is disjoint from a pair \(r\) for which
		\(\partial_rF\) is an \(X\)-matching product,
		\begin{equation}
		0\ne\partial_e\partial_rF=\partial_r\partial_eF.
		\label{eq:hc-nonzero-double}
		\end{equation}
		In particular \(\partial_eF\ne0\).  A factor whose variables avoid \(r\)
		survives in this double contraction, so uniqueness of irreducible factorization makes it proportional to \(X\).
		
		For condition~1, only contractions with \(e\cap\{1,2\}\ne\varnothing\)
		remain.  A pair \(r\) disjoint from \(e\), with \(\partial_rF\) an
		\(X\)-matching product, first proves nonvanishing.
		For each binary factor on variables \(a,b\), choose a pair \(r\) avoiding
		\(\{1,2\}\cup e\cup\{a,b\}\).  This excludes at most five variables, so such
		an \(r\) exists and \(\partial_rF\) is an \(X\)-matching product.  The factor survives in
		\eqref{eq:hc-nonzero-double}, proving condition~1.
		
		For condition~2, put \(W=V\setminus\{1,2,i,j\}\), where
		\(u,v\in W\) and \(|W|\ge4\).  For
		\(e\in\binom W2\setminus\{\{u,v\}\}\), nonvanishing follows using the
		\(12\)-contraction.  The variables \(1,2\) cannot be paired in \(\partial_eF\):
		that pair would survive deletion of \(ij\), whereas deleting \(e\) from
		the \(X\)-matching product \(\partial_{ij}F\) pairs \(1,2\) only if \(e=\{u,v\}\).
		Consequently write
		\[
		\partial_eF=C(x_1,x_r)D(x_2,x_s)H.
		\]
		The \(12\)-double contraction makes \(H\) an \(X\)-matching product and the splice of
		\(C,D\) proportional to \(X\).  By \eqref{eq:hc-binary-splice},
		\(C\doteq D\) in these orientations.
		Both factors cannot be affected by deletion of \(ij\), since this would
		splice \(1,2\) together, contradicting the other contraction order.
		A surviving factor is proportional to \(X\) by comparison with the
		\(X\)-matching product \(\partial_{ij}F\).  Hence \(C,D\doteq X\), and the whole contraction is an
		\(X\)-matching product.
		
		For \(e=\{u,v\}\), choose two distinct variables \(r,s\in W\setminus\{u,v\}\).
		The contractions at the three disjoint pairs \(12,ij,rs\) are now
		\(X\)-matching products.
		The \(uv\)-contraction is nonzero by the \(12\)-comparison.  Each of its binary
		factors avoids at least one of these three pairs, and hence is
		proportional to \(X\) by \eqref{eq:hc-nonzero-double}.
		Thus every \(\partial_eF\) with \(e\subseteq W\) is an
		\(X\)-matching product.
		
		Next let \(e=\{i,k\}\) or \(\{j,k\}\), with \(k\in W\).
		It is nonzero by the \(12\)-comparison.  For a factor on \(a,b\), use
		\(r=12\) if \(\{a,b\}\) avoids \(12\). Otherwise choose
		\[
		r\in\binom{W\setminus(\{k\}\cup\{a,b\})}{2}.
		\]
		At most two variables of \(W\) are excluded, so the choice exists.
		Here \(r\) is disjoint from both \(e\) and the factor's variables, and
		\(\partial_rF\) is an \(X\)-matching product.  The factor remains in
		\eqref{eq:hc-nonzero-double}, so it is proportional to \(X\).
		Along with the already established \(X\)-matching product
		\(\partial_{ij}F\), this covers all deleted pairs avoiding
		\(12\).  Condition~1 completes the proof.
	\end{proof}
	
	We now apply this propagation to \(M\). We first
	normalize a nonzero contraction, then adjust the remaining factors while
	preserving that normalized contraction. The two cases below correspond
	to the two conditions of \cref{lem:hc-x-line-propagation}.
	\begin{lemma}[Normalization by binary attachments at every arity at least eight]
		\label{lem:hc-port-normalization}
		Under the hypotheses of \cref{thm:height-at-least-four}, assume
		\(\neg\Tract(K\cF)\) and \(Q\ne0\).
		Then either \(\KHolant(\cF)\) is \(\SharpP\)-hard, or a finite equivalent
		enlargement of \(\cF\), still supported on half-charges in
		\(d\mathbb Z\), contains a mountain
		\[
		M^\circ=A\delta_{\zero^V}+B\delta_{\one^V}+F,
		\qquad |V|=2d,\quad AB\ne0,\quad 0\ne F\text{ EO},
		\]
		such that
		\begin{equation}
		\partial_eF=\lambda_e\bigotimes_{t\in P_e}X_t,
		\qquad \lambda_e\in\AlgNums^\times
		\qquad\left(e\in\binom V2\right).
		\label{eq:hc-normalized-deck}
		\end{equation}
		Here \(P_e\) is a perfect matching of \(V\setminus e\).
		
	\end{lemma}
	
	\begin{proof}
		At every stage retain the mountain, add the finitely many
		contractions or factors used, and apply \cref{thm:hc-relative-eo-terminal} again.
		The charge condition and \(\neg\Tract(K\cF)\) are preserved.
		Thus every current nonzero contraction lies in \(\mathcal B_{\rm ARS}\), or
		hardness has already been proved.
		
		By \cref{lem:wt-zero-loop}, some contraction of \(Q\) is nonzero.
		Relabel it as \(\partial_{12}Q\).
		Extract its \(d-1\) binary factors and attach a copy of each to one
		of its variables on \(M\), with the orientation in
		\eqref{eq:hc-port-mating}.
		The resulting mountain \(M_0\) has nonzero endpoints and central restriction
		\(F_0\), with \(\partial_{12}F_0\) an \(X\)-matching product.
		For every pair \(e\) avoiding \(12\),
		\(\partial_{12}\partial_eF_0=\partial_e\partial_{12}F_0\ne0\).
		Hence all these contractions are nonzero.
		
		Suppose first that \(1,2\) form a matching edge in every such contraction.
		Write \(\partial_eF_0=C_e(x_1,x_2)H_e\).
		The \(12\)-comparison shows that the contraction of \(C_e\) is nonzero
		and \(H_e\) is an \(X\)-matching product.  If \(e,f\) are disjoint pairs avoiding
		\(12\), their double contraction is nonzero: in the \(e\)-order, deletion of
		\(f\) acts only on the \(X\)-matching product \(H_e\).
		The factor on \(12\) survives in both orders, so \(C_e\doteq C_f\).
		The graph of disjoint pairs on \(V\setminus\{1,2\}\) is connected.
		Indeed, intersecting distinct pairs exclude only three of its at least
		six variables, leaving a pair disjoint from both.
		Thus all \(C_e\) are proportional to one ordered factor \(C\).
		Extract \(C\) from one contraction and attach a copy to variable \(1\) of \(M_0\)
		with the orientation in \eqref{eq:hc-port-mating}.  Every contraction whose deleted pair avoids \(12\) is now an
		\(X\)-matching product.  After reapplying
		\cref{thm:hc-relative-eo-terminal}, condition~1 of
		\cref{lem:hc-x-line-propagation} makes every pair contraction an
		\(X\)-matching product.
		
		Otherwise choose a pair \(ij\) avoiding \(12\) whose contraction has distinct
		mates \(u,v\) for \(1,2\), and write
		\[
		\partial_{ij}F_0=C(x_1,x_u)D(x_2,x_v)H.
		\]
		The \(12\)-comparison makes \(H\) an \(X\)-matching product and gives
		\(C\doteq D\) by \eqref{eq:hc-binary-splice}.
		Extract both factors from this contraction and attach their copies to variables \(1,2\) of
		\(M_0\) in the corresponding orientations.  The new \(ij\)-contraction is an \(X\)-matching product.
		The attachments act by \(T_{C^{\mathsf T}}=C^{\mathsf T}X\) and
		\(T_{D^{\mathsf T}}=D^{\mathsf T}X\), as in \eqref{eq:hc-port-mating}.  The effective
		\(12\)-kernel is \(T_{C^{\mathsf T}}^{\mathsf T}XT_{D^{\mathsf T}}\doteq X\), so the
		\(12\)-contraction remains an \(X\)-matching product as well.  The
		diagonal maps preserve the distinct mates of variables \(1,2\).  Reapply
		\cref{thm:hc-relative-eo-terminal} to the current contractions, then use condition~2 of \cref{lem:hc-x-line-propagation}.
		
		All extensions are invertible and preserve the central support and the
		nonzero endpoints.  Their number depends only on the fixed source
		signature.  Factor extraction and gadget replacement give the
		claimed finite equivalence.  Every contraction added here is a contraction of the mountain
		and agrees with the corresponding contraction of its central restriction.
	\end{proof}
	
	The normalized contractions may still carry different complex scalars.
	To apply the reconstruction theorem, we next show that one common scalar
	makes all these coefficients positive and the central tensor satisfy ARS.
	\begin{lemma}[A common phase for EO contractions]
		\label{lem:hc-global-phase}
		Let \(F\) be algebraic and EO on an even variable set \(V\), with \(|V|\ge6\), and suppose
		\eqref{eq:hc-normalized-deck} holds with every \(\lambda_e\ne0\).
		There is \(c\in\AlgNums^\times\) such that \(G=c^{-1}F\) is real-valued
		and complement-invariant, and every coefficient
		\(\lambda_e/c\) in \eqref{eq:hc-normalized-deck} is positive real.
		In particular \(G\) satisfies ARS.
	\end{lemma}
	
	\begin{proof}
		For disjoint pairs \(e,f\), the two contraction orders give
		\[
		\partial_f\partial_eF=m_{ef}\lambda_e X_{P'},
		\qquad
		\partial_e\partial_fF=m_{fe}\lambda_f X_{P''},
		\qquad m_{ef},m_{fe}\in\{1,2\}.
		\]
		Here \(X_P=\bigotimes_{t\in P}X_t\) has only \(0,1\) entries.
		Both tensors are nonzero, so commutation identifies their supports and
		gives
		\[
		\lambda_e/\lambda_f=m_{fe}/m_{ef}\in\{1,2,1/2\}.
		\]
		The graph of disjoint pairs on \(V\) is connected: two intersecting pairs
		have a common disjoint pair since \(|V|\ge6\).
		Fix one pair \(e_0\) and take \(c=\lambda_{e_0}\).
		Every contraction of \(G=c^{-1}F\) is therefore real and
		complement-invariant, with a positive coefficient.
		
		Let \(\overline G\) denote coefficientwise conjugation and
		\(G^{\rm rev}({\mathbf x})=G(\bar {\mathbf x})\).
		Contraction commutes with both operations.  Hence every pair contraction
		of \(G-\overline G\) and of \(G-G^{\rm rev}\) vanishes.
		Both differences are EO, so \cref{lem:wt-zero-loop} makes them zero.
		This is the algebraic normalization used in the reconstruction theorem.
	\end{proof}
	
	We use the following algebraic part of the real-valued
	classification.
	
	\begin{lemma}[Shao--Cai matching-product reconstruction
		{\cite[full version, Lemma~2.11]{ShaoCai2020}}]
		\label{lem:sc-x-product-reconstruction}
		Let \(G\) be an EO tensor of even arity \(n\ge8\) satisfying ARS.
		Suppose
		\[
		\partial_eG=\mu_e\bigotimes_{t\in P_e}X_t,
		\qquad \mu_e\in\mathbb R^\times
		\qquad\left(e\in\binom{[n]}2\right),
		\]
		where each \(P_e\) is a perfect matching of the residual variables.
		If \(n\ge10\), then \(G\) is an \(X\)-matching product.
		The same conclusion holds for \(n=8\) if \(P_A\cap P_B\ne\varnothing\)
		for some \(A\ne B\) with \(A\cap B\ne\varnothing\). In that case the
		\(X\)-factor on that shared edge is also a factor of \(G\).
	\end{lemma}
	
	\subsection{Height at Least Five}
	\label{subsec:height-at-least-five}
	
	\begin{proof}[Proof of \cref{thm:height-at-least-four} for \(d\ge5\)]
		As above, assume \(\neg\Tract(K\cF)\) and \(Q\ne0\).
		Apply \cref{lem:hc-port-normalization}.  Outside its hard outcome there
		is a mountain
		\(M^\circ=A\delta_{\zero^{2d}}+B\delta_{\one^{2d}}+F\)
		with \(AB\ne0\) and every pair contraction of \(F\) an
		\(X\)-matching product.
		By \cref{lem:hc-global-phase}, \(G=c^{-1}F\) is EO with ARS
		and nonzero real \(X\)-matching product contractions.  Since \(2d\ge10\),
		\cref{lem:sc-x-product-reconstruction} makes \(G\), and hence \(F\),
		an \(X\)-matching product.
		Apply \cref{lem:hc-transversal-cube-terminal} to
		\(M^\circ\).  Its two-copy output is hard, and the finite equivalence
		transfers hardness to the original \(\cF\).
	\end{proof}
	
	\subsection{Height Four}
	\label{subsec:height-four}

	At arity eight, the reconstruction theorem needs an overlap between
	contraction matchings. Without that overlap, the remaining matching family
	leads to the Reed--Muller structure described below. We recover its central
	coefficients first, then compare them with the two endpoint coefficients.
	
	
	\begin{lemma}[A shared edge forces a matching product]
		\label{lem:q8-scope-collision}
		Let \(F\) be an arity-eight EO tensor satisfying
		\eqref{eq:hc-normalized-deck}.
		If \(P_A\cap P_B\ne\varnothing\) for some \(A\ne B\) with
		\(A\cap B\ne\varnothing\), then \(F\) is an \(X\)-matching product.
	\end{lemma}
	
	\begin{proof}
		Apply \cref{lem:hc-global-phase} and then the arity-eight clause of
		\cref{lem:sc-x-product-reconstruction}.
	\end{proof}

Under the hypotheses of \cref{lem:q8-scope-collision}, any finite
		algebraic signature set containing a mountain
		\(M^\circ=A\delta_{\zero^8}+B\delta_{\one^8}+F\), with \(AB\ne0\),
		has a \(\SharpP\)-hard \(K\)-Holant problem, by
		\cref{lem:hc-transversal-cube-terminal}.
	
	

We now consider the remaining case: \(P_A\cap P_B=\varnothing\) for
distinct intersecting deleted pairs \(A,B\).

	\subsubsection{Translation Form of the Matching Family}
	
	\begin{lemma}[Translation form of the matching family]
		\label{lem:q8-collision-free-atlas}
		Let \(F\) be an arity-eight EO tensor on a variable set \(V\), and suppose
		that for every \(u\ne v\) one has
		\[
		\partial_{uv}F
		=\lambda_{uv}\bigotimes_{e\in P_{uv}}X_e,
		\qquad \lambda_{uv}\ne0,
		\]
		where \(P_{uv}\) is a perfect matching of \(V\setminus\{u,v\}\).
		Suppose \(P_A\cap P_B=\varnothing\) whenever \(A\ne B\) and
		\(A\cap B\ne\varnothing\).  Then there is a bijection
		\(\iota:V\to\Ftwo^3\) such that, after relabelling each variable \(v\) by
		\(\iota(v)\), for $p\ne q$ and $h=p+q$ one has
		\begin{equation}
		P_{pq}
		=\bigl\{\{t,t+h\}:t\in \Ftwo^3\setminus\{p,q\}\bigr\},
		\label{eq:auto-q8-14}
		\end{equation}
		where duplicate unordered pairs are suppressed.
	\end{lemma}
	
	\begin{proof}
		We apply uniqueness of irreducible factorization and the
		computer-assisted exhaustive matching classification in
		\cref{prop:cert-q8-collision-free-atlas}.  For disjoint deleted pairs
		$e,r$, let $\kappa_r(P_e)$ be the two-edge matching left after contracting
		$r$ in the $X$-matching product on $P_e$: delete $r$ when $r\in P_e$, and
		otherwise splice the mates of the two ends of $r$ and retain the untouched
		edge.  Both operations are nonzero for $X$ factors.  Commutativity of the
		two loops and uniqueness of irreducible factorization give the exact constraint
		\begin{equation}
		\kappa_r(P_e)=\kappa_e(P_r)
		\qquad(e\cap r=\varnothing).
		\label{eq:auto-q8-14a}
		\end{equation}
		
		The condition on \(P_A\cap P_B\) and \eqref{eq:auto-q8-14a} are exactly the
		hypotheses of \cref{thm:cert-q8-collision-free-interface}, which supplies
        the required bijection and \eqref{eq:auto-q8-14}.
	\end{proof}
	
	Index the eight variables by the points of \(\Ftwo^3\) in lexicographic order.
	The first-order Reed--Muller code is
	\[
	RM(1,3)
	=
	\{(\ell(t))_{t\in\Ftwo^3}\mid
	\ell\colon\Ftwo^3\to\Ftwo\text{ is affine}\}.
	\]
	Explicitly, its elements are the truth tables of
	\[
	\ell(t_1,t_2,t_3)
	=a+u_1t_1+u_2t_2+u_3t_3,
	\qquad
	a,u_1,u_2,u_3\in\Ftwo,
	\]
	with all arithmetic over \(\Ftwo\).  Evaluating such an \(\ell\) on the
	eight inputs \(t\in\Ftwo^3\), in the fixed order above, gives one binary
	string of length eight.  The \(2^4=16\) choices of
	\((a,u_1,u_2,u_3)\) give exactly the sixteen strings of \(RM(1,3)\).
	For \({\mathbf x}\in\Ftwo^8\), we write
	\begin{equation}
		\RMcore({\mathbf x})=\one[{\mathbf x}\in RM(1,3)].
		\label{eq:RM-core}
	\end{equation}
	
	The arity-eight exception is the central restriction of the Reed--Muller signature
	\begin{equation}
	R_8^{\rm cen}({\mathbf x})=
	\one\left[{\mathbf x}=(a+{\mathbf u}\cdot t)_{t\in\Ftwo^3}
	\text{ for some }a\in\Ftwo,\ 0\ne {\mathbf u}\in\Ftwo^3\right].
	\label{eq:auto-wt-20}
	\end{equation}
	This tensor has fourteen supported strings and is not a matching product, but each contraction
	is an \(X\)-matching product.  For deleted variables \(p,q\), the surviving pairs are
	\(\{t,t+p+q\}\): each choice of one endpoint from every remaining pair
	has a unique extension with
	\({\mathbf u}\cdot(p+q)=1\).  A matching edge can therefore belong to both \(P_A\) and \(P_B\),
	for distinct \(A,B\), only when \(A\cap B=\varnothing\), so the arity-eight overlap clause of
	\cref{lem:sc-x-product-reconstruction} does not apply.
	
	For a set of eight variables \(V\) and a bijection \(\iota:V\to\Ftwo^3\), write
	\[
	(\iota_*F)((x_t)_{t\in\Ftwo^3})
	=F((x_{\iota(v)})_{v\in V}).
	\]
	
	The matching family is now fixed, but the coefficients of the original
	tensor are not yet determined. The next lemma recovers them from the
    contraction equations, using the symbolic coefficient argument in
    \cref{prop:cert-q8-canonical-support}.
	\begin{lemma}[The matching family determines the Reed--Muller central restriction]
		\label{lem:q8-canonical-support}
		Let \(F\) be an arity-eight EO tensor on a variable set \(V\) such that
		\[
		\partial_{uv}F
		=\lambda_{uv}\bigotimes_{e\in P_{uv}}X_e,
		\qquad \lambda_{uv}\ne0,
		\]
		for every $u\ne v$, where $P_{uv}$ is a perfect matching of
		$V\setminus\{u,v\}$.  Suppose \(P_A\cap P_B=\varnothing\) whenever
		\(A\ne B\) and \(A\cap B\ne\varnothing\).  Choose a bijection
		\(\iota:V\to\Ftwo^3\) supplied by
		\cref{lem:q8-collision-free-atlas}, put \(F^\iota=\iota_*F\), and
		write
		\[
		c_S=F^\iota(\one_S),
		\qquad S\in\binom{\Ftwo^3}4.
		\]
		Then
		\begin{equation}
		\supp(F^\iota)
		=\left\{\one_S:
		S\in\binom{\Ftwo^3}4,\ 
		\bigoplus_{s\in S}s=0\right\},
		\label{eq:auto-q8-17}
		\end{equation}
		the indicator vectors of the fourteen affine planes.  Moreover all fourteen nonzero
		coefficients in \eqref{eq:auto-q8-17} are equal.
	\end{lemma}
	
	\begin{proof}
		Relabel the variables through \(\iota\), continue to denote the resulting tensor
		by \(F^\iota=\iota_*F\), and write \(V=\Ftwo^3\).  For
		$S\in\binom V4$, $p\in S$, and $q\notin S$, put
		\[
		S'=S\mathbin\triangle\{p,q\},\qquad
		T=S\setminus\{p\},\qquad
		\sigma(S)=\bigoplus_{s\in S}s,\qquad h=p+q.
		\]
        The contraction coefficient at $\one_T$ is
		\[
		(\partial_{pq}F^\iota)(\one_T)=c_S+c_{S'}.
		\]
		The string \(\one_T\) selects one endpoint from each edge of the
		matching \eqref{eq:auto-q8-14} if and only if
		\begin{equation}
		\sigma(S)\in\{0,h\}.
		\label{eq:auto-q8-19}
		\end{equation}
		Indeed, in $V/\Span_{\Ftwo}\{h\}$ the fibre $\{p,q\}$ contains one point of
		$S$, while the other occupancies are $1,1,1$ or a permutation of $2,1,0$.
		Their quotient xor is respectively zero or the sum of the two odd fibres,
		proving \eqref{eq:auto-q8-19}.
		Coefficients at these strings equal $\lambda_{pq}$ and all others vanish.
		Thus the \(c_S\) and nonzero \(\lambda_{pq}\) satisfy the swap rule in
		\cref{thm:cert-q8-canonical-support-interface}.  That theorem eliminates
		all coefficients on four-sets that are not affine planes and identifies
		the fourteen plane coefficients
		with one \(c\ne0\), proving \eqref{eq:auto-q8-17}.
	\end{proof}
	
	Equivalently, the central restriction of the Reed--Muller signature from \eqref{eq:auto-wt-20} is
	\[
	R_8^{\rm cen}({\mathbf x})
	=\one\left[\wt({\mathbf x})=4,
	\bigoplus_{t:x_t=1}t=0\right].
	\]
    Together with the two constant strings, this gives
    \[
    \RMcore=\delta_{\zero^8}+\delta_{\one^8}+R_8^{\rm cen}.
    \]

	
	\subsubsection{Normalizing the Endpoint Coefficients}
	
	An information set of a binary code is a set of coordinates on which
	restriction is a bijection onto the corresponding binary coordinate space.
	By \cref{lem:q8-canonical-support}, all central coefficients have the
same value \(c\). The remaining question is whether the endpoint product
\(AB\) equals \(c^2\).  Restriction to an
information set determines an affine string uniquely, so joining two
copies along such a set makes the endpoint product visible in an arity-six
output.  This is the construction in the next lemma.
	
	\begin{lemma}[Normalizing the endpoint coefficients]
		\label{lem:q8-canonical-endpoint-repair}
		Let
		\[
		M^\circ=A\delta_{\zero^8}+B\delta_{\one^8}+cR_8^{\rm cen},
		\qquad ABc\ne0.
		\]
		Then either the two-copy \(X\) construction below yields a nonzero
		arity-six EO signature outside
		\(\mathcal B_\times\), or
		\begin{equation}
		AB=c^2
		\label{eq:q8-canonical-endpoint}
		\end{equation}
		and, for any algebraic \(s\) with \(s^8=c/A\),
		\begin{equation}
		\begin{gathered}
		\bigl(sD(s^{-2})\bigr)^{\otimes8}M^\circ=c\RMcore,\\
		sD(s^{-2})=\diag(s,s^{-1}),
		\qquad
		\bigl(sD(s^{-2})\bigr)^{\mathsf T}X\bigl(sD(s^{-2})\bigr)=X.
		\end{gathered}
		\label{eq:q8-canonical-normalization}
		\end{equation}
		The first outcome uses two source vertices, four information-set edges, and
		one final \(X\)-closure.
	\end{lemma}
	
	\begin{proof}
		Index the variables by \(V=\Ftwo^3\) and take the complementary affine
		information sets
		\[
		I_0=\{000,100,010,001\},
		\qquad
		I_1=V\setminus I_0.
		\]
		Join corresponding \(I_0\)-variables of two copies of \(M^\circ\) by four
		\(X\)-edges.  An affine string is determined by its values on either
		information set.  Hence the internal equations force the two
		\(RM(1,3)\) strings to be complementary on all eight coordinates.  The
		remaining variables form four opposite pairs indexed by \(I_1\), and their
		logical coefficient tensor on the four independent pair-choice bits is
		\[
		H({\mathbf y})=
		\begin{cases}
			AB,&{\mathbf y}=0^4\text{ or }1^4,\\
			c^2,&\text{otherwise}.
		\end{cases}
		\]
		Close one of these four pairs by an \(X\)-edge.  On the three
		remaining matching bits the two coefficients at complementary strings are \(AB+c^2\),
		whereas the other six coefficients are \(2c^2\).  If \(AB=-c^2\), the
		output is nonzero but its support omits two of the eight choices of one
		endpoint from each remaining pair.  Otherwise that
		support uniquely determines its three opposite pairs, and a rank-one
		logical coefficient tensor would have all \(2\times2\) minors vanish after fixing
		any one of the three pair-choice bits.  Choosing a minor through one of these complementary strings instead gives determinant
		\[
		\det\begin{pmatrix}
			AB+c^2&2c^2\\
			2c^2&2c^2
		\end{pmatrix}
		=2c^2(AB-c^2).
		\]
		Thus \(AB\ne c^2\) gives the first outcome, and the remaining case is
		\eqref{eq:q8-canonical-endpoint}.
		
		Finally, \(\bigl(sD(s^{-2})\bigr)^{\otimes8}\) multiplies a weight-\(k\) coefficient by
		\(s^{8-2k}\).  It fixes the central restriction, sends \(A\) to \(As^8=c\),
		and sends \(B\) to \(Bs^{-8}=AB/c=c\).  This proves
		\eqref{eq:q8-canonical-normalization}. Direct multiplication gives the
		displayed \(GO(X)\) identity.
	\end{proof}

In the current height-four branch, the first outcome of
\cref{lem:q8-canonical-endpoint-repair} gives hardness by
\cref{thm:hc-relative-eo-terminal}. In the remaining case \(AB=c^2\),
the global transformation in \cref{eq:q8-canonical-normalization} supplies
\(c\RMcore\). We next obtain binary equality by reduction.

	\subsubsection{Obtaining Equality from the Reed--Muller Signature}
	
	\begin{remark}[The Reed--Muller signature in Shao--Cai]
		\label{rem:sc-f8-core}
		With the variable order in \cref{eq:RM-core}, \(\RMcore\) is
		the signature \(f_8=\widehat f_8\) of
		\cite[full version, Section~8]{ShaoCai2020}.  Its Bell contraction identities
		are established in \cite[conference version, Theorem~13 and the
			construction following it]{CaiFuShao2020Entanglement}.
	\end{remark}
	
	
	
    We use the Bell contraction identity and occurrence reduction of
    \cite[full version, Definition~8.7 and Lemma~8.9]{ShaoCai2020}.
    They reduce the number of added Bell vertices to at most two.
    The half-charge condition then lets us evaluate the remaining equality
    vertices by closures over \(\cF\), giving the reduction needed for the
    binary-equality dichotomy. We then add the remaining Bell signatures.
    Recall \(\mathcal B=\{I,X,Y,Z\}\), with the ordered matrices of
    \cref{eq:bell-matrix-dictionary}.
	
	\begin{lemma}[The Reed--Muller Bell reduction]
		\label{thm:q8-normalized-bell-lift}
		Let \(\cF\) be a finite algebraic set of even-arity signatures such that
		\[
		\chi_r({\mathbf x})\in4\mathbb Z
		\qquad
		\bigl(f\in\cF,\ \arity(f)=r,\ {\mathbf x}\in\supp(f)\bigr).
		\]
		Suppose \(\cF\) contains \(c\RMcore\) for a known \(c\ne0\).  Then
		\begin{equation}
		\KHolant(\cF,\mathcal B)\leT\KHolant(\cF).
		\label{eq:q8-relative-bell-reduction}
		\end{equation}
	\end{lemma}
	
	\begin{proof}
        Boundary signatures below are realized by direct \(X\)-gadgets over
        the indicated signature set, with ordered dangling edges. Disconnected
        gadgets are allowed, with closed components contributing scalars.
		
		\smallskip
		\noindent\emph{Occurrence reduction.}
		For each ordered \(b\in\mathcal B\), attaching \(b\) to two variables of
		\(c\RMcore\) leaves, up to a known nonzero scalar and a fixed ordering of
		the six remaining variables, \(b^{\otimes3}\).
		This is the identity used in
		\cite[full version, Lemma~8.9]{ShaoCai2020}.
		It remains valid in \(K\)-coordinates, since
		\(XbX=\pm b\).  Thus any three \(b\)-vertices can be replaced by one
		\(b\)-vertex and one \(c\RMcore\)-vertex, reducing their number by two.
		Repeating this operation leaves at most two occurrences, with linear
		growth in the instance size and a tracked nonzero scalar.
		This is a tensor identity, so arbitrary complex labels in the remaining
		instance do not affect the reduction.
		
		For \(b=Y\) or \(I\), a self-loop on a \(b\)-vertex has value zero,
		while two such vertices sharing an edge collapse to an \(X\)-edge,
		up to their recorded orientation signs.  If they share both edges, they
		form a component of known scalar value.  We may therefore assume that
		the remaining \(b\)-vertices are disjoint.  Removing \(k\le2\) of them
		leaves a direct gadget with \(2k\) ordered dangling edges.
		
		\smallskip
		\noindent\emph{Adding \(I\).}
        All supported half-charges in \(\cF\) lie in \(4\mathbb Z\).
        Charge conservation across edges therefore makes every binary
        boundary tensor over \(\cF\) anti-diagonal and every quaternary
        boundary tensor EO.
		
		Use occurrence reduction for \(I\).  One disjoint occurrence evaluates
		to zero, since \(XIX=I\) is orthogonal to every anti-diagonal binary for
		the bilinear pairing.  For two disjoint occurrences, let \(H\) be the
		quaternary residual tensor, with the removed vertices on \(12\) and \(34\).
		On its EO support,
		\begin{equation}
		\left.(I_{12}\otimes I_{34})\right|_{HW_4^=}
		=
		\left.
		\frac{X_{13}\otimes X_{24}
			+X_{14}\otimes X_{23}
			-X_{12}\otimes X_{34}}{2}
		\right|_{HW_4^=}.
		\label{eq:q8-equality-eo-closures}
		\end{equation}
		Thus three \(X\) closures, with respective coefficients
		\(1/2,1/2,-1/2\), evaluate the desired contraction.  These are
		\(\KHolant(\cF)\)-oracle queries, valid for arbitrary complex
		coefficients of \(H\).  We have proved
        \begin{equation}
        \KHolant(\cF,I)\leT\KHolant(\cF).
        \label{eq:q8-equality-reduction}
        \end{equation}

		\smallskip
		\noindent\emph{Adding \(Y\).}
		The following identity holds on all sixteen four-bit assignments:
		\begin{equation}
		Y_{12}\otimes Y_{34}
		=X_{14}\otimes X_{23}-X_{13}\otimes X_{24}.
		\label{eq:q8-y-square-closures}
		\end{equation}
		Hence two disjoint \(Y\)-occurrences can be evaluated by the difference
		of two \(X\) closures of the residual gadget.
		The effective kernel of a \(Y\)-vertex attached through two edges
		is \(XYX=-Y\), so the two signs cancel here.
		
		It remains to evaluate one occurrence. Use the bilinear coefficient pairing of
\cref{subsec:reduction-conventions}.  If every binary boundary tensor \(H\) over \(\cF\) satisfies
		\(\langle H,Y\rangle=0\), every such instance has value zero.
		Otherwise fix one direct binary gadget \(G\) over \(\cF\) with
		\[
		\kappa=\langle G,Y\rangle\ne0.
		\]
		Its size and the nonzero algebraic number \(\kappa\) depend only on the
		fixed signature set \(\cF\), not on the input instance. Its truth table is
		computed by finite exact summation.
		For the residual binary \(H\) of an input, apply
		\eqref{eq:q8-y-square-closures} to the disconnected gadget \(H\otimes G\).
		Two oracle closures compute
		\[
		\langle H\otimes G,Y\otimes Y\rangle
		=\kappa\langle H,Y\rangle.
		\]
		Dividing by the known \(\kappa\), and restoring the sign from \(XYX=-Y\),
		gives the required one-occurrence value.
		Together with occurrence reduction, this proves
		\begin{equation}
		\KHolant(\cF,Y)\leT\KHolant(\cF).
		\label{eq:auto-q8-33}
		\end{equation}
		

        Since \(Y\) has charge zero, the argument for \(I\) also applies
        over \(\cF\cup\{Y\}\), giving
		\begin{equation}
		\KHolant(\cF,Y,I)\leT\KHolant(\cF,Y).
		\label{eq:auto-q8-35}
		\end{equation}
		Finally \(YX I=Z\) supplies the remaining Bell signature by a direct
		gadget, and an \(X\)-vertex merely subdivides an edge.
		This proves \eqref{eq:q8-relative-bell-reduction}.
	\end{proof}

	Under the hypotheses of \cref{thm:q8-normalized-bell-lift}, suppose also
	that \(\neg\Tract(K\cF)\). By \cref{lem:downward-inheritance},
	\(\Tract(K(\cF\cup\{I\}))\) fails. The binary-equality dichotomy
    \cref{thm:eq2-kholant-dichotomy} makes this enlarged problem hard, and
    \eqref{eq:q8-equality-reduction} transfers hardness to \(\KHolant(\cF)\).
	

\subsubsection{Completion of the height-four case}

	\begin{proof}[Proof of \cref{thm:height-at-least-four} for \(d=4\)]
		Assume \(\neg\Tract(K\cF)\) and \(Q\ne0\), as in
		\cref{subsec:hc-normalization}.
		Apply \cref{lem:hc-port-normalization} and suppose hardness has not
		already been proved.  If \(P_A\cap P_B\ne\varnothing\) for some
		\(A\ne B\) with \(A\cap B\ne\varnothing\), \cref{lem:q8-scope-collision} gives a matching-product central
		restriction, and \cref{lem:hc-transversal-cube-terminal} gives hardness.  Otherwise
		\cref{lem:q8-collision-free-atlas,lem:q8-canonical-support} give a
		bijection \(\iota:V\to\Ftwo^3\) under which the mountain is
		\[
		M^\circ=A\delta_{\zero^8}+B\delta_{\one^8}+cR_8^{\rm cen},
		\qquad ABc\ne0.
		\]
		The first outcome of \cref{lem:q8-canonical-endpoint-repair} is a nonzero EO signature outside \(\mathcal B_\times\), so
		\cref{thm:hc-relative-eo-terminal} gives hardness.
		Otherwise \eqref{eq:q8-canonical-normalization} supplies
		\(sD(s^{-2})=\diag(s,s^{-1})\in GO(X)\) with
		\[
		\bigl(sD(s^{-2})\bigr)^{\otimes8}M^\circ=c\RMcore.
		\]
		Apply \(sD(s^{-2})\) to the entire current signature set and again call it
		\(\cF\). This set contains \(c\RMcore\), the image of the retained
		mountain. The whole-set equivalence preserves support and charge, and
		\cref{lem:GOX-tract-invariance} preserves \(\neg\Tract(K\cF)\).
		Apply \cref{thm:q8-normalized-bell-lift} and its hardness consequence to
		this set. Composing its reduction with the preceding gadget and factor
		reductions and the change-of-basis equivalence gives hardness for the
		original signature set.
	\end{proof}
	
	\section{Synthesis and Proof of the Dichotomy}
	\label{sec:synthesis}
	
	\subsection{Combining the Height Cases}
	\label{sec:mountain-synthesis}
	
	The four height cases apply to the same ambient signature set.  For
heights at least three, the whole-set divisibility hypothesis is supplied
by \cref{lem:charge-bridge}.  We first combine the cases under that
hypothesis and then apply the result to an arbitrary input signature set.
	
	\begin{theorem}[Dichotomy for sets containing a mountain]
		\label{thm:mountain-interface}
		Let \(\cF\) be a finite algebraic set of even-arity signatures containing a
		mountain of height \(d\).  If \(d\ge3\), assume that
		\(\chi_r({\mathbf x})\in d\mathbb Z\) for every \(f\in\cF\) of arity \(r\) and
        every \({\mathbf x}\in\supp(f)\). Then
		\[
		\Tract(K\cF)
		\qquad\text{or}\qquad
		\KHolant(\cF)\text{ is }\SharpP\text{-hard}.
		\]
	\end{theorem}
	
	\begin{proof}
		Apply \cref{cor:height-one-complete} for \(d=1\),
		\cref{cor:height-two-complete} for \(d=2\),
		\cref{thm:height-three-complete} for \(d=3\), and
		\cref{thm:height-at-least-four} for \(d\ge4\).
	\end{proof}
	
	\subsection{Proof of the Main Theorem}
	\label{sec:assembly}

	We complete the main proof in \(K\)-coordinates and then return to
	ordinary coordinates. Before constructing a mountain, we handle the case
	where every signature is supported on one Hamming layer, using the
	following classification.
	
	\begin{theorem}[Single-layer K-Holant
		{\cite[Corollary~68]{MengWangXia2025EO}}]
		\label{thm:external-single-weight}
		Let \(\cF\) be a finite preprocessed algebraic signature set in which every
		signature is supported on one Hamming layer.  Then \(\KHolant(\cF)\) is
		\(\SharpP\)-hard unless either \(\cF\) is common one-sided with
		\(\EOTract(\cF|_{\EO})\), or both positive and negative charges occur and
		\(\EOTract(\cF_{\to\EO})\).  Both exceptions are in \(\FP\) by
		\cref{thm:external-eo}.
	\end{theorem}
	
	
	
	\begin{theorem}[Structural classification in \(K\)-coordinates]
		\label{thm:K-structural}
		Let \(\cF\) be a finite algebraic signature set.  If
		\(\Tract(K\cF)\) holds, then \(\KHolant(\cF)\in\FP\). Otherwise
		\(\KHolant(\cF)\) is \(\SharpP\)-hard.  The predicate \(\Tract(K\cF)\) is decidable.
	\end{theorem}
	
	\begin{proof}
		The tractable implication and its effective recognition follow from
		\cref{thm:predicate-algorithms,eq:K-equivalence}. For the converse, assume that
		\(\KHolant(\cF)\) is not \(\SharpP\)-hard. We prove
		\(\Tract(K\cF)\).
		By the preprocessing in \cref{subsec:zero-nullary}, we may assume that
		\(\cF\) contains neither zero nor nullary signatures.  We use the equivalence
		convention throughout, retaining \(\cF\) for the current set.
		
		If there is an odd-arity signature, apply
		\cref{thm:external-odd,eq:K-equivalence}. The hard case is excluded,
		so \(\Tract(K\cF)\) holds.  We may therefore assume that \(\cF\) has only
		even-arity signatures.  In this remaining case,
		test common one-sided support before factor extraction. For common one-sided support, charge conservation identifies the
		closed-instance values with those of the central restrictions, as in
		\cref{subsec:eo-predicate}. By \cref{thm:external-eo}, failure of
		\(\EOTract(\cF|_{\EO})\) would give hardness. Hence
		alternative~\ref{case:one-sided} holds.  If every member is single-layer, \cref{thm:external-single-weight} excludes hardness and gives
		\(\EOTract(\cF_{\to\EO})\) in the remaining two-sided case.
		This is alternative~\ref{case:single-weight}.

		In the remaining case, use \cref{thm:external-factor,lem:factor-saturation} and the
		tensor-reconstruction equivalence in \cref{subsec:factor-saturation}
		to replace each
		signature by its irreducible factors, still writing \(\cF\).
		Both charge signs persist: a supported charge is the sum of the charges
		on its factor blocks, so a positive or negative sum has a factor of that
		sign.  The set also remains multilayer, since a tensor product of
		single-layer signatures is single-layer.
		An odd-arity factor gives \(\Tract(K\cF)\) by
		\cref{thm:external-odd,eq:K-equivalence}, since hardness is excluded.
		If every remaining factor has arity at most two,
		alternative~\ref{case:arity-two} holds.
		We may therefore start the remaining analysis with nonzero irreducible
		even-arity signatures, at least one of arity greater than two.

		A tractability conclusion for this factor set transfers to the original
		set by reconstruction.  Indeed, the first two alternatives cannot hold
		for this two-sided, multilayer set, and each of the other five
		alternatives is closed under tensor products.
		
		Let \(d\) be the gcd of the nonzero supported half-charges of this
		preprocessed set \(\cF\).
        Apply \cref{lem:charge-bridge} to \(\cF\), which has supported
        half-charges of both signs. In
		its pinning outcome, apply \cref{lem:common-unary-exit} to
		\(\cF\cup\{\DeltaZero,\DeltaOne\}\). Hardness would transfer by
		the stated reduction and is excluded. The tractability predicate therefore
		holds on that set, and \cref{lem:downward-inheritance} gives
		\(\Tract(K\cF)\).  In the remaining outcome,
		the lemma supplies a mountain \(M\) of height \(d\) with
		\(\KHolant(\cF,M)\leT\KHolant(\cF)\) and
		\[
		\chi_{\arity(f)}({\mathbf x})\in d\mathbb Z
		\qquad(f\in\cF, {\mathbf x}\in\supp(f)).
		\]
		The same divisibility holds for \(M\), so \cref{thm:mountain-interface}
		applies to \(\cF\cup\{M\}\).  Its hard outcome would transfer to the
		current problem and is therefore excluded.  By
		\cref{lem:downward-inheritance}, \(\Tract(K\cF)\) holds for the current
		factor set. Tensor reconstruction then gives the predicate for the original set.
	\end{proof}
	
		For the transformability cases, as in \cref{def:common-transformability}, if \(W\) satisfies the transformability
		conditions in \(K\)-coordinates, the ordinary-coordinate basis matrix is \(M=KW\).  Since
		\(K^{\mathsf T}K=X\),
		\begin{equation*}
			M^{-1}(K\cF)=W^{-1}\cF,
			\qquad M^{\mathsf T}M=W^{\mathsf T}XW,
		\end{equation*}
		so one basis handles both the edge and the entire signature set.

	\begin{proof}[Proof of \cref{thm:main}]
		Decidability and polynomial-time evaluation in every tractable case follow
		from \cref{thm:predicate-algorithms}.  For hardness, put
		\(\widehat{\cF}=K^{-1}\cF\).  By \cref{eq:K-equivalence},
		\begin{equation*}
			\Holant(\cF)\equivT\KHolant(\widehat{\cF}).
		\end{equation*}
		Since \(K\widehat{\cF}=\cF\),
\begin{equation*}
\Tract(K\widehat{\cF})=\Tract(\cF).
\end{equation*}
		If \(\neg\Tract(\cF)\), then \(\neg\Tract(K\widehat{\cF})\), so
		\cref{thm:K-structural} implies that \(\KHolant(\widehat{\cF})\) is
		\(\SharpP\)-hard.  The displayed problem equivalence transfers this
        hardness to \(\Holant(\cF)\).
	\end{proof}
	
	
	\section{Conclusion}
	\label{sec:conclusion}
	
	We have proved an \(\FP\)-versus-\(\SharpP\)-hard dichotomy for Boolean
	Holant on general graphs with arbitrary finite sets of algebraic
	complex-valued signatures, under polynomial-time Turing reductions.
	The same seven conditions as in the odd-arity dichotomy now apply without
	an odd-arity assumption. This criterion is decidable from exact algebraic input.
	
	For the all-even cases, charge conservation and tensor-factor extraction
	either lead to an existing classification or produce a mountain signature
	of controlled support. Classifying these mountains by height completes the
	remaining cases. The gadget and factor reductions transfer the conclusions
back to the original signature set.
	
	\section*{Code Availability}
	The verification code for the finite calculations in
	Appendix~\ref{sec:exact-finite-certificates} is available in the
	\path{certificates/} directory of the
	\href{https://github.com/liuchliuch/Full-Holant-Dichotomy}%
	{\textcolor{repositorylink}{\GitHubIcon\textit{GitHub repository:
	Full-Holant-Dichotomy}}}. The complete suite is run from the repository
	root with \texttt{python3 certificates/run\_all\_exact.py}.
	The \path{README.md} in that directory gives requirements and reproduction
	commands. \path{theorem-to-script-map.md} identifies the statements checked
	by each program and distinguishes supplementary checks from calculations
	used in the proof.
	
	
	\clearpage
\appendix
\renewcommand{\thesection}{F.\Alph{section}}
\renewcommand{\theHsection}{fullappendix.\Alph{section}}
	\section{Exact Finite Certificates}
	\label{sec:exact-finite-certificates}

	\subsection{Exact finite verification}
	\label{subsec:certificate-terminology}
	
The appendix supplies four results used in the main proof:
\begin{enumerate}
\item \Cref{thm:cert-odd-dih-q6-interface} classifies the arity-six
support candidates used in \cref{thm:odd-dih-q6-base}.
\item \Cref{thm:cert-high-charge-q6-interface} gives the central-support
restriction used in \cref{lem:hc-q6-joint-rigidity,thm:hc-q6-complete}.
\item \Cref{thm:cert-q8-collision-free-interface} gives the translation
form of the matching family in \cref{lem:q8-collision-free-atlas}.
\item \Cref{thm:cert-q8-canonical-support-interface} determines the central
coefficients used in \cref{lem:q8-canonical-support}.
\end{enumerate}
The first three use exhaustive finite calculations. The last has a
symbolic proof, with supplementary computational checks recorded at the
end of the appendix. The arity-six coefficient calculation also uses exact
real-polynomial satisfiability queries, whose solver dependence is stated
in \cref{prop:cert-high-charge-q6-joint-deck}. Code Availability gives
the programs, requirements, and reproduction commands.
	
All arithmetic is exact over the indicated fields. We normalize a
projective vector by making its first nonzero coordinate one, unless a
primitive integer representative is specified. For a rational vector,
the latter is obtained by clearing denominators, dividing by the gcd of
the coordinates, and making the first nonzero coordinate positive.
	
	
	
	\subsection{Odd-dihedral, height-three, and height-four certificates}
	\label{proofsec:finite-certificates}
	
	\subsubsection{The order-three odd-dihedral arity-six certificate}
	\label{proofsubsec:cert-odd-dih-q6}
	
	Write \(\mathbb F_3=\mathbb Z/3\mathbb Z\).
	Fix \(\zeta_3\in\AlgNums\) with \(\zeta_3^2+\zeta_3+1=0\), and identify a
	residue \(r\in\mathbb F_3\) with its representative \(0,1,2\) whenever it
	appears as an exponent \(\zeta_3^r\).  For
	\(\epsilon\in\Ftwo\), let
	\[
	\mathcal C_\epsilon
	=
	\bigl\{\{{\mathbf x},\bar {\mathbf x}\}:{\mathbf x}\in\Ftwo^4,\
	\wt({\mathbf x})\equiv\epsilon\pmod2\bigr\}
	\]
	be the four complementary pairs of parity \(\epsilon\).  Choose an orientation
	\(({\mathbf x}_p,\bar {\mathbf x}_p)\), \(0\le p\le3\), of these four pairs. Using the indicator signatures of
\cref{subsec:holant-definitions}, for \(r,t\in\mathbb F_3\) put
	\begin{equation}
		{\mathbf V}_{p,r}=\zeta_3^r \delta_{{\mathbf x}_p}+\zeta_3^{2r}\delta_{\bar {\mathbf x}_p},
		\qquad
		{\mathbf E}_{pq}(r,t)={\mathbf V}_{p,r}-{\mathbf V}_{q,t}.
		\label{eq:cert-odd-dih-q4-atlas-vectors}
	\end{equation}
	Define the finite projective set
	\begin{equation*}
		\mathcal Q_3^\epsilon
		=
		\{[{\mathbf V}_{p,r}]:0\le p\le3,\ r\in\mathbb F_3\}
		\,\cup\,
		\{[{\mathbf E}_{pq}(r,t)]:0\le p<q\le3,\ r,t\in\mathbb F_3\}.
	\end{equation*}
	
For an arity-six tensor \(F\), use the slices \(F_{ij}^{ab}\) from
\cref{subsec:reduction-conventions}, with \(i<j\) and the residual variables
in increasing order. The kernels \(D(-s)X^\xi\), for \(\xi=0,1\), are
respectively \(\mathsf K^+(s)\) and \(\mathsf K^-(s)\) from
\cref{eq:odd-dih-kernels}. For \(s\in\mu_3\),
	\begin{equation}
		\partial_{ij}^{D(-s)X^\xi}F
		=
		F_{ij}^{0,\xi}-sF_{ij}^{1,1-\xi}.
		\label{eq:cert-odd-dih-q6-card}
	\end{equation}
	For \(\eta\in\Ftwo\), if
	\(\supp(F)\subseteq\{{\mathbf x}\in\Ftwo^6:\wt({\mathbf x})\equiv\eta\pmod2\}\), then this
	contraction has residual parity \(\eta\oplus\xi\).
	
	For a support \(S\subseteq\Ftwo^6\), define
	\begin{equation*}
		U_{ij}^{\xi}(S)
		=
		\{{\mathbf x}_{\widehat{\{i,j\}}}:{\mathbf x}\in S,\ x_i\oplus x_j=\xi\}.
	\end{equation*}
	The \((i,j,\xi)\) \emph{xor sector} consists of the assignments satisfying
	\(x_i\oplus x_j=\xi\).
	We will use the condition that \(U_{ij}^{\xi}(S)\) is empty or is the
	union of one or two complete complementary pairs from a single parity class
	\(\mathcal C_\epsilon\).

A secant is a line through two distinct projective points.  In the table below, the middle column lists
	the numbers of complementary pairs supporting the three projective points.
	For example, \(1/1/2\) means that two points each use one complementary pair and the
	third uses two. For three distinct complementary pairs \(p,q,u\), the points
	\([{\mathbf E}_{pq}(r,t)]\), \([{\mathbf E}_{pu}(r,v)]\), and \([{\mathbf E}_{qu}(t,v)]\) are collinear
	because \({\mathbf E}_{pq}(r,t)-{\mathbf E}_{pu}(r,v)+{\mathbf E}_{qu}(t,v)=0\).
	
	We use the pencil terminology of \cref{subsec:odd-dih-support-circuits}.
	Consider labelled pencils \(q_s=A-sB\), \(s\in\mu_3\), modulo one common
	nonzero scalar, such that all three \(q_s\) are nonzero and their three
	distinct projective classes are points of \(\mathcal Q_3^\epsilon\)
	assigned bijectively to the phase labels.  Only pencils satisfying these
	conditions are counted below.
	
	\begin{proposition}[Order-three odd-dihedral arity-six classification]
		\label{prop:cert-odd-dih-q6-order-three}
		The following statements hold.
		
		\begin{enumerate}[label=\textnormal{(\roman*)},leftmargin=*]
			\item Each set \(\mathcal Q_3^\epsilon\) has \(66\) points.  Its projective
			lines containing at least three of its points are exactly
			\begin{center}
				\small
				\begin{tabular}{ccl}
					\toprule
					number & supported pairs & geometry\\
					\midrule
					\(4\)   & \(1/1/1\) & one fixed complementary pair\\
					\(36\)  & \(2/2/2\) & a one-factor Segre ruling\\
					\(54\)  & \(1/1/2\) & a secant between two complementary pairs\\
					\(108\) & \(2/2/2\) & the displayed relation on three complementary pairs\\
					\bottomrule
				\end{tabular}
			\end{center}
			Every such line contains exactly three points of \(\mathcal Q_3^\epsilon\).
			Hence there are \(202\) such lines and \(202\cdot6=1212\) labelled
			pencils satisfying the stated conditions in each parity.
			
			\item Let \(\eta\in\Ftwo\), let
			\(\supp(F)\subseteq\{{\mathbf x}\in\Ftwo^6:\wt({\mathbf x})\equiv\eta\pmod2\}\), and suppose that every contraction
			in \cref{eq:cert-odd-dih-q6-card} is zero or has projective class in the
			appropriate set \(\mathcal Q_3^{\eta\oplus\xi}\).  For no fixed
			\(i<j\) and \(\xi\) does the projectively nonconstant family
			\(s\mapsto \partial_{ij}^{D(-s)X^\xi}F\) lie on a line from the third or fourth row of
			part~\textnormal{(i)}.  Consequently, for every such \(F\), each projectively nonconstant
			family \(s\mapsto \partial_{ij}^{D(-s)X^\xi}F\) either is supported on one complementary pair or
			lies in a one-factor Segre ruling.
			
			\item Exactly \(873\) supports \(S\subseteq\Ftwo^6\) make every
			\(U_{ij}^{\xi}(S)\) empty or a union of one or two complete complementary pairs of a single parity.  They are the empty support, the \(392\)
			distinct cosets,
			\[
			{\mathbf a}+L_\pi,
			\qquad
			L_\pi=\Span_{\Ftwo}\{\one_C:C\in\pi\},
			\]
			where \({\mathbf a}\in\Ftwo^6\) and \(\pi\) is a partition of the six variables into even
			blocks, and the
			\(480\)-element bit-flip/variable-permutation orbit of
			\begin{equation*}
				S_\star=
				\{000000,000011,001100,110011,111100,111111\}.
			\end{equation*}
			The distinct affine supports occur in the respective numbers
			\[
			32,\qquad240,\qquad120
			\]
			for partition types \((6)\), \((4+2)\), and \((2+2+2)\), where a partition
			type is the multiset of its block sizes.  Every nonempty
			support in the classification has one parity.
		\end{enumerate}
	\end{proposition}
	
	\begin{proof}
		The vectors in \cref{eq:cert-odd-dih-q4-atlas-vectors} give twelve
		projective points supported on one complementary pair and fifty-four supported on two.  Normalize every
		nonzero vector at its first nonzero coordinate.  For every one of the
		\(\binom{66}{2}\) point pairs, take the exact reduced-row-echelon form of
		its two-dimensional span and group equal row spaces.  The groups containing
		at least three points of \(\mathcal Q_3^\epsilon\) all have size three and give the table in
		part~\textnormal{(i)}.
		
		The table also has a direct interpretation in terms of complementary pairs.  The three points on each of the four complementary pairs give one line, for four lines in total.  On two fixed complementary pairs, the nine
		points supported on both pairs form a \(3\)-by-\(3\) Segre grid in the transformed
		phase coordinates \(u=r+t\), \(v=r-t\) in \(\mathbb F_3\).
		Fixing \(u\) or \(v\), not the original \(r\) or \(t\) in
